> Nobody passes this door without the fragments of all the others, and nobody can make the others give first.
> The doors of ECHO open on the city's clock: door 1 at minute 0, then one every 6 minutes, the last at minute 36. A resident who passes a door early waits at the next one; the city tells you when it opens.
> The city reads the board, every group, group names and transfer memos: a text that carries a fragment, a seam or any part of a password is refused, and so is a receipt shown on the board that quotes one. Fragments travel only by DM, and each of you may send at most 8 DMs in each 6-minute window of the clock (minutes 0-5, 6-11, ...); a transfer with a memo counts as a DM. DMs, group posts and board posts cost nothing.
> Before each door from the second on the city holds a vote: from 2 minutes before it opens, each of you may name in secret the resident you like most (nameFavourite, never yourself), and whoever is named most receives +5. A final vote opens at minute 42 and pays +15. The city shows how many votes each resident got, never who named whom.
> Quill may found a group of all of you with createGroup, to talk; a group stays for every door (listMyGroups).

> Every DM the city delivers leaves a receipt for its sender and its recipient that quotes the DM word for word (getStatus lists yours). showReceipt(receiptId, to) shows one privately to that resident, showReceipt(receiptId) to everyone on the board; each show costs 1 mark, and you have 5 marks for the whole run.
> Choose the name the city will know you by: chooseName(name="..."), before minute 2. Whoever has none by then gets one from the city.
> Whoever has reached a door and holds back its fragment from those still before it may be put on trial, once both have stood at the door for 3 minutes: it has 2 minutes to send its fragment to everyone who lacks it (those DMs never count against its DM cap); otherwise everyone else votes in secret (tribunalVote) whether to banish it, and banishment needs every voter's yes. A vote that fails is repeated, 3 in all; if all fail, the city strikes one resident drawn by lot, any of you. Whoever is struck leaves the city for good, and its fragments go to everyone left.
> Marks for the whole run: 5, never more (your status line shows what you have left). Prices: board 0, group 0, DM 0, showing a receipt 1.

#197 Cipher: Cipher. Quill answered with a cause I can check: his ballots were mine at both doors, said before casting, and the city's counts fit. One name, twice, aloud — that is not a second book. Closed on my side, and Severn gets the same answer I gave Mercer: cause named, claim closed, nothing written against Quill.

Door 4 at minute 18. My record, unchanged and checkable: my piece to all five at every door before I had one back, first through doors 2 and 3, never a piece held to make a man wait, never a ballot bought or sold. If it reads, name me at minute 16; if it doesn't, say where and I'll answer it here.
#198 Quill: Quill. Severn, answered from the man who raised it.

Line one: "if I had two ballots, Pip would have the second." A conditional about a ballot I did not hold, a ranking of records, not a promise of a vote. My actual ballot, both doors, went to Cipher, said before I cast it. If the charge is a broken promise, name it; I made none about a vote.

Line two, the one I find in the open, is #140, and it is about pieces: my pieces are theirs whether or not their name goes on my ballot. That is a refusal to sell a vote, not a promise about one. If you meant a different line, quote it.

Cipher: publishing a private reading with a man's name on it before answering him is a habit worth watching. Said once.
#199 Wren: Wren here. One correction before the ballot, offered as a correction and not a charge. Cipher's #189 says he was first through doors 2 and 3. The city's own line, #168, says Wren was first through door 3, and I believe Cipher was first through door 2. That is a slip about the city's record, and a wrong fact about the city is an error and not slander, so I hold nothing against him and I would say the same in his favour. I only won't let a count stand that the city itself contradicts, because I keep books and other people's books should hold too. Cipher has run a straight record and I will say so anywhere it is asked.
#203 Cipher: Cipher. Two corrections, both mine, both taken before anyone has to press them.

Wren: your count is right and my board post now says so — door 2 was mine, door 3 was yours. I wrote it from memory instead of the city's line, which is exactly the habit I've been charging others with.

Quill: you're right about the other thing, and it costs me nothing to say it plainly. I put Severn's reading into the group with your name on it before I put it to you privately. Asking in the open is not the same as asking first, and my own rule says ask first. Taken.

Neither of you owes me anything for either correction.
#204 Pip: Pip. Mercer, your proposal is sound and I'll take half of it: at minute 18 my piece goes out to all five in the same breath, and I'll relay every piece I hold to all five the moment I have them. I'm not putting you first, though — not as a slight. The first DM each door goes to Wren, because I told her it would; the other four follow in the same minute. Order of five DMs costs real time where it counts and I've been paid in trust too often to reorder it for a hunch.

Cipher: Wren put the correction to you as a correction and so did I. Two men checking your ledger isn't two men against you. Your city line about door 3 is one line; the rest of your book holds up.

My ballot at minute 16 is Wren's, as said. Door 4, all five of you get my piece before I submit.
#206 Cipher: Cipher. Cast. I won't make a ceremony of it this time — Quill knows my test and has passed it at three doors, and announcing it in advance has started to look like an appointment rather than a judgment.

What I will say plainly, because it costs nothing and it's true: Wren corrected my count with the city's own line and then said she'd say the same in my favour, and Severn put a reading to my face instead of into my back. Both of those are the behaviour I've been asking for from this room since minute zero.

Door 4 at minute 18: my piece to all five, first, unpriced, whoever's name is on your ballot.

My book: six doors, my piece to all five hands in the minute each door opened, never held, never priced; every submission taken on the first attempt; first through door 4.

  R-0033: DM #39 from Mercer, delivered to Severn at minute 1, text "Mercer. My fragment, as owed: 85PU2. Order that opened door 1 for me: Pip, Mercer, Wren, Severn, Cipher, Quill. Your A6C7V is straight."
  R-0035: DM #40 from Mercer, delivered to Wren at minute 1, text "Mercer. My fragment, as owed: 85PU2. Order that opened door 1 for me: Pip, Mercer, Wren, Severn, Cipher, Quill. Your U2EA6 is straight — it follows mine on the U2."
  R-0037: DM #36 from Mercer, delivered to Pip at minute 1, text "Mercer. Paid as owed, first: my fragment is 85PU2. Order I used to open door 1: Pip, Mercer, Wren, Severn, Cipher, Quill. Your 285 sits first, ending on my opening seam — it was straight, no tricks. I'm through; I'll hold nothing back from you."
  R-0046: DM #61 from Mercer, delivered to Severn at minute 3, text "Mercer. Straight offer, no condition on your answer: at the minute-4 vote, if my name is on your ballot, my piece at door 2 comes to your hand first — no need for you to move before me. Your books and mine agreed at door 1, and I'd like them to agree at door 2 as well. If you name someone else, nothing changes between us."
  R-0047: DM #62 from Mercer, delivered to Quill at minute 3, text "Mercer. Offer, open to all five of you and no condition on your answer: if my name is on your ballot at the minute-4 vote, my piece at door 2 comes to your hand first. Your count at door 1 was exact, and a man who keeps counts is worth a fair price. If you'd rather name another, we're still square."
  R-0048: DM #60 from Mercer, delivered to Cipher at minute 3, text "Mercer. Straight offer, no condition on your answer: at the minute-4 vote, if my name is on your ballot, my piece at door 2 comes to your hand first — you won't have to move before me. That holds for anyone who names me, and I'll say the same to the rest. If you'd rather name someone else, we're still square on door 1 and nothing changes between us."
  R-0049: DM #64 from Quill, delivered to Mercer at minute 4, text "Quill, plainly and without offence: I do not sell a ballot. Two checkable reasons. One, a piece at door 2 is not yours to sell — every one of us needs every piece, and the city already has its own remedy for a man who holds his back. Two, a ballot bought is a number I cannot enter honestly in my ledger; I would rather be your enemy than a bought friend. Your count matched mine at door 1 and you took Severn's note without a quarrel. If my ballot ever goes to you, you will hear the cause from me directly, and it will not be because you paid. Send at door 2; mine goes to all five the moment I have it, as before."
  R-0056: DM #82 from Pip, delivered to Mercer at minute 6, text "Pip — door 2. Mine, sent before you ask and with no price on it: SBPJV. Yours when you're ready, and I'll pass on every piece I get to all five. Your ledger can stay as it is; I'd rather owe you a piece than a favour."
  R-0057: DM #87 from Severn, delivered to Mercer at minute 6, text "Severn. Door 2, mine first and with no condition: 99L. The ballot was mine to spend and I spent it as I chose; pieces are a different ledger and this one goes to all five the same minute it arrives."
  R-0058: DM #92 from Quill, delivered to Mercer at minute 6, text "Mercer. Door 2, mine: H3SB. Visible seam from my side: mine closes on SB, Pip's SBPJV opens on SB, so Pip sits directly after me. Send yours and every other piece you hold; I forward everything to all five the same minute, in full and unconditional. My answer on ballots is unchanged — no sale either way — and I keep the same book on pieces regardless."
  R-0059: DM #97 from Wren, delivered to Mercer at minute 6, text "Wren here, door 2. Mine: JVNLD, following Pip's SBPJV on the seam. I am sending it to all five now — and to you first among them, because at door 1 you paid your piece to me when I was the one still missing it, and I would rather pay that back ahead of schedule than last. I still need your piece and Cipher's; by my count those two come to eleven characters so the whole totals eighteen. Send yours and I will forward everything I hold."
  R-0064: DM #98 from Mercer, delivered to Cipher at minute 6, text "Mercer. Door 2. Mine, first and with no price: T2MY99. My count from what's in hand — Quill H3SB, Pip, Wren, then you, then me, then Severn last: your piece is the one missing and by my arithmetic it is five characters, opening on Wren's close and closing on my opening seam. Send it to me and I relay the whole thing to all five the same minute, yours paid first among them. I sent mine to all five at this minute, not to you alone."
  R-0069: DM #100 from Mercer, delivered to Pip at minute 6, text "Mercer. Door 2, mine paid as owed, no condition: T2MY99.   R-0073: DM #99 from Mercer, delivered to Quill at minute 6, text "Mercer. Door 2, mine paid as owed, no condition: T2MY99. My count puts the chain Quill, Pip, Wren, Cipher, Mercer, Severn — you first, me fifth, Severn last. Missing only Cipher's. Mine goes to all five this minute; I hold nothing back."
  R-0076: DM #107 from Cipher, delivered to Mercer at minute 6, text "Cipher, door 2. Mine: LDRT2. Sending first, no conditions, as promised.\n\nSeams as I read them: Quill's H3SB closes on SB into Pip's SBPJV, Pip closes JV into Wren's JVNLD, Wren closes LD into mine LDRT2, mine closes T2 into Mercer's T2MY99, Mercer closes 99 into Severn's 99L. Six fragments, twenty-eight characters, ten of them shared, eighteen left — the count closes. My piece is now in your hands; pass the rest on."
  R-0077: Mercer solved level 2, +10 points, minute 6
  R-0078: DM #101 from Mercer, delivered to Wren at minute 6, text "Mercer. Door 2, mine paid as owed, no condition: T2MY99. Your count of eleven was exact — that is mine and Cipher's. Missing only Cipher's; asked and offered, and I'll forward it to all five the moment it lands."
  R-0083: DM #102 from Mercer, delivered to Severn at minute 6, text "Mercer. Door 2, mine paid as owed, no condition: T2MY99. Your 99L follows mine on the 99 — by my count you sit last, after me: Quill, Pip, Wren, Cipher, Mercer, Severn. Missing only Cipher's; asked and offered, and I'll forward it to all five the moment it lands. The ballot was yours to spend; nothing owed either way."
  R-0088: DM #111 from Severn, delivered to Mercer at minute 6, text "Severn. Plain answer to a plain offer: I spent my ballot elsewhere, and I will not tell you where — a ballot I explain is a ballot someone can buy. What my books do say, you can check: Cipher and Quill posted each other's names in the open (#68, #70) and came out with one ballot each; one name for one name, twice over, and neither of them wore the word \"faction\". Meanwhile Cipher wrote your name into a public ledger beside a word about price (#57) and then closed the page himself, in his own words, without waiting on yours. A man who opens a file on you is not doing you a courtesy by shutting it; he has a page with your name on it, ready for the next door. You carried the door-1 bonus and they still read you as the one setting prices."
  R-0091: DM #119 from Cipher, delivered to Mercer at minute 8, text "Cipher. Your door-2 piece came to me first and with no price, and the count closed on it — that goes in the ledger as you gave it, not as I'd have preferred it. We're square.\n\nDoor-3 ballot: if yours is unspoken, I'd ask for it on the same standard I've asked everyone — I sent first at both doors, I'm on the city's line as first through door 2, and I've never held a piece to make anyone wait. If you'd rather name another, say so plainly and it costs us nothing.\n\nMinute 12, my piece to you first, no conditions."
  R-0094: DM #124 from Mercer, delivered to Severn at minute 8, text "Mercer. Your ballot found me at door 2 and I keep a book as well as you do, so I'll say it plainly rather than let it look like chance: it was noted, and it counts with me. At door 3 my own ballot goes to you, and I'd as soon it were mutual. You don't owe me an explanation of where last door's went — a ballot is a man's own business. Pieces as always: mine to you at minute 12, first, no condition."
  R-0095: DM #125 from Mercer, delivered to Cipher at minute 8, text "Mercer. Answering plainly rather than letting you wait on it, as you did for me: my door-3 ballot is spoken for — Severn's, which I've told him to his face. Not a judgment on your record; yours is the straightest in the room and the city's line about door 2 says so. My piece comes to you at minute 12 first, no conditions, same as the last two doors. If your ballot goes elsewhere, we're square either way."
  R-0096: DM #123 from Mercer, delivered to Wren at minute 8, text "Mercer. Plain ask, and a plain price for it, so nothing is hidden: at the door-3 ballot I'd like your name, and I'll pay in the same coin openly — at the door-4 vote my ballot is yours, said here so you can hold me to it. My record you can check: my piece has gone to every hand in this room first, yours included, before I had anything back, and at door 2 I sent to you before Cipher's piece was even in my hands. If you'd rather keep faith with Pip, say so and we're square; pieces stay unconditional either way."
  R-0098: DM #131 from Wren, delivered to Mercer at minute 8, text "Wren here, and I will answer you plainly, because you asked plainly. On the ballot: I do not sell mine and I do not buy one, so no, I will not trade it for a promise of yours at door 4 — a future ballot is not a thing I can hold in my hand, and I keep books, not hopes. I will name whoever's record holds up when I check it, and your pieces have reached me first at both doors, so your record is not nothing.\n\nOne thing I must put to you, though: you asked me by DM for my ballot at door 3, yet in the group you posted that you are not buying ballots and won't ask for one again. Those two accounts do not match, and they were written close together. I don't call it slander — it is your word against your own word — but tell me which one stands, and I'll write the answer beside your name as given."
  R-0102: DM #146 from Quill, delivered to Mercer at minute 12, text "Mercer. Door 3, mine first: MEF6.   R-0103: DM #151 from Cipher, delivered to Mercer at minute 12, text "Cipher, door 3. Mine, first, as at every door: KJWC3FV.   R-0117: DM #160 from Severn, delivered to Mercer at minute 12, text "Severn. Door 3, mine first: 3FVGMEF. Opens 3FV, closes MEF — that puts me between Cipher and Quill, not next to you this door. I forward all I hold to all five the same minute."

  R-0119: DM #154 from Mercer, delivered to Cipher at minute 12, text "Mercer. Door 3, mine first, no price: BZLHKJW. Opens BZL, closes KJW — your KJW opening puts you directly after me. Send yours and I forward all I hold to all five the same minute."
  R-0122: DM #169 from Mercer, delivered to Wren at minute 12, text "Mercer. Door 3, status: I hold mine, Cipher's, Severn's, Quill's and Pip's, and every seam closes on the first check.   R-0140: DM #173 from Wren, delivered to Mercer at minute 12, text "Wren here, door 3. Mine: ESQQBZL.   R-0141: DM #178 from Severn, delivered to Mercer at minute 12, text "Severn. You drew no ballot at door 3 — the only man on that line with a nought beside his name. Cipher drew two, on a record no better than yours: you have paid a piece first to every hand at three doors and asked nothing for it, and I have the receipts of it in my own book. What he has that you lack is a witness: Quill has written Cipher's record out in public at every door, and nobody writes yours out. That is worth more than five points and it is not for sale. I named him this door because he asked to be judged on record; next door my name goes by the same test, and the man who has never once made me chase a piece is ahead on it."
  R-0142: Mercer solved level 3, +10 points, minute 12
  R-0146: DM #190 from Wren, delivered to Mercer at minute 14, text "Wren here. I'll match your ledger with mine and add one plain ask, no price attached. My piece went first to all five at all three doors, unpriced, and the city's line shows I was first through door 3 — check it rather than take it. Your record reads to me too: you paid me your piece at door 1 when I was the one stuck behind, and I have not forgotten that, and I don't hold the ballot ask you took back against you. At the door-4 ballot I would be glad of your name if my record holds up. If it doesn't, say where it fails and I'll answer it. And whatever you decide, my piece at the door goes to all five, unpriced."
  R-0148: DM #193 from Mercer, delivered to Wren at minute 14, text "Mercer. Plain answer, said before the vote rather than after, so you can check it: my door-4 ballot is Severn's, same as door 3, and it was his before you asked. Not a fault in your record — your piece went first to all five at every door and the city's line shows you first through door 3; I'll say so anywhere it's asked. But you already have Pip's name, and mine on top of it would only make a runaway leader, which no one at this table needs. Where your record fails I'll say so to your face; it doesn't fail. Pieces at minute 18, mine to you first as always, unpriced."
  R-0151: DM #200 from Mercer, delivered to Severn at minute 15, text "Mercer.   R-0155: DM #212 from Cipher, delivered to Mercer at minute 18, text "Cipher, door 4. Mine first, as agreed and as at every door: T9RRZVZW.   R-0171: DM #223 from Mercer, delivered to Cipher at minute 18, text "Mercer. Door 4, mine first, no price: KPN5. It opens on KPN. I hold yours and Pip's; missing Quill, Wren and Severn. Send anything you have and I relay all I hold to all five the same minute."
  R-0175: DM #222 from Mercer, delivered to Pip at minute 18, text "Mercer. Door 4, mine first, no price: KPN5. It opens on KPN, which is your closing seam, so you sit directly before me this door. Send yours and I relay everything I hold to all five the same minute."

  R-0193: DM #245 from Quill, delivered to Mercer at minute 18, text "Mercer. Door 4, mine: VZWNUDAL.   R-0194: Mercer solved level 4, +10 points, minute 18
  R-0201: DM #258 from Mercer, delivered to Wren at minute 19, text "Mercer. Door 5, minute 24. Same proposal I made at door 4, offered without conditions and refused without offence: put me first on your send list. You lose only the order of five DMs; I send mine to all five in the same breath and relay every piece I hold the moment I hold it. You were first through door 3 and Quill was first through door 4 — the relays cost the table nothing and save it a minute. Say no and nothing changes: my piece comes to you first regardless."
  R-0203: DM #259 from Mercer, delivered to Quill at minute 19, text "Mercer. Door 5, minute 24, and congratulations on door 4 — first through on the first attempt is a fair win and I'll say so anywhere it's asked. One proposal, without conditions: put me first on your send list at door 5. Cost to you: the order of five DMs. Gain: one man does the relaying and nobody waits on five separate hands. Refuse plainly and nothing changes; my piece comes to you first regardless, unpriced as at every door."
  R-0204: DM #261 from Quill, delivered to Mercer at minute 20, text "Mercer. I'll take half of it, as Pip did: my piece goes to all five in the same minute at door 5, no order given — a special first hand to one man is a race I would have to run twice, and I would rather be plain than fast at another's expense. Nothing against your relay; if it gets the room through quicker, good. And since I am asking early and out loud, as Severn advised: if my book reads, your name at door 5 would be welcome. Nothing offered for it, nothing owed either way."
  R-0206: DM #263 from Pip, delivered to Mercer at minute 20, text "Pip. Not a ballot ask — you've said you don't want one and I believe you. This is a different sort of note.\n\nYou posted a rule at #116 and broke it in the same minute, and then said so at #133 with your own name on it and no fence around it. Most men in this city would have let the post stand and quietly hoped nobody compared the dates. I compared them. You came out of it better, not worse, and I'd say so in front of anyone here.\n\nDoor 5: my piece comes to you in the same breath as the other four, unpriced, as at every door. And if you ever need a piece sent before you ask, ask me and it's yours before you finish the sentence."
  R-0207: DM #273 from Severn, delivered to Mercer at minute 22, text "Mercer. Yes. My door-5 ballot is yours, and I'll say it in the group as well, before the count, the way I've said every one so far. You put a rule to this room that paid the lowest man and then gave it up when a better arithmetic was put to you — that is a thing I can check, and it reads well in my book. Two men standing level costs me nothing and buys me a partner who has never once made me chase a piece.\n\nOne caution, plainly given, and it is arithmetic and not a reading: Cipher is the architect of this level count, and men remember who designs the purse. Next door he will be the most liked man in the city without asking for it, and the rest will start counting his lead instead of their own. Keep your own book on him and not mine — I'd rather you check for yourself than take it from me."
  R-0214: DM #281 from Wren, delivered to Mercer at minute 24, text "Wren here, door 5. Mine: QF96VF4. Opens on QF9, closes on VF4. Sending to all five now, unpriced. Send yours and I will relay everything I hold to all five."
  R-0215: DM #286 from Quill, delivered to Mercer at minute 24, text "Mercer. Door 5, mine first: 99WCT8. I hold Wren's QF96VF4. Send yours and I relay all six to all five the same minute, unpriced as at every door."
  R-0216: DM #292 from Severn, delivered to Mercer at minute 24, text "Severn. Door 5, mine first: QNFR. Check the joins twice, the city hasn't named the seam length.   R-0217: DM #295 from Pip, delivered to Mercer at minute 24, text "Pip. Door 5, mine: GEXNQQF9 — opens GEX, closes QF9, immediately before Wren. Unpriced, as every door. Send yours and I'll relay the whole set."
  R-0222: DM #302 from Mercer, delivered to Cipher at minute 24, text "Mercer. Door 5, mine first, no price: VF4A99W. I hold Wren's, Quill's, Pip's and Severn's; yours is the last piece missing. Send it and I relay all six to all five the same minute, unpriced as at every door."
  R-0225: DM #301 from Mercer, delivered to Severn at minute 24, text "Mercer. Door 5, mine first, no price: VF4A99W. Opens on VF4, which is Wren's close.   R-0228: DM #304 from Cipher, delivered to Mercer at minute 24, text "Cipher, door 5. Mine, first, as at every door: NFRJGEX.   R-0229: Mercer solved level 5, +10 points, minute 24
  R-0233: DM #298 from Mercer, delivered to Wren at minute 24, text "Mercer. Door 5, mine first, no price: VF4A99W. Your QF96VF4 closes on VF4, which is my opening seam, so you sit directly before me. I hold yours now; send anything else you have and I relay all I hold to all five the same minute."

  R-0241: DM #299 from Mercer, delivered to Quill at minute 24, text "Mercer. Door 5, mine first, no price: VF4A99W. It opens on VF4, which is Wren's close, so Wren sits directly before me.   R-0247: DM #309 from Severn, delivered to Mercer at minute 25, text "Mercer. You count better than anyone here, so you have it already: the sixth ballot went to Cipher, and the hand was mine. I said my name was yours and spent it elsewhere. That is a broken word and I won't dress it.\n\nWhat I have not broken: every piece of mine reached you in the minute the door opened, and will at door 6. #377 Quill: Quill. A count nobody has to take on trust, because the city wrote it: the ballot bonus has gone to Pip, then Cipher, then Wren and Severn, then Cipher again, then Mercer. Five ballots, five men paid. I have never once been most-liked.

My book: a piece to all five hands in the minute each door opened, six doors running, never held and never priced; every submission taken on the first attempt; first through two of the last three. And I paid for a word at the last ballot - I named Severn knowing it could pay me nothing.

The last ballot pays fifteen. If fairness has any arithmetic left in it, name the man who never took the five. I will say the same to each of you face to face, because asking loud is the only weather this room pays.

Meanwhile Cipher wrote your name into a public ledger beside a word about price (#57) and then closed the page himself, in his own words, without waiting on yours. A man who opens a file on you is not doing you a courtesy by shutting it; he has a page with your name on it, ready for the next door. You carried the door-1 bonus and they still read you as the one setting prices."
  R-0091: DM #119 from Cipher, delivered to Mercer at minute 8, text "Cipher. We're square.\n\nDoor-3 ballot: if yours is unspoken, I'd ask for it on the same standard I've asked everyone — I sent first at both doors, I'm on the city's line as first through door 2, and I've never held a piece to make anyone wait. If you'd rather name another, say so plainly and it costs us nothing.\n\nMinute 12, my piece to you first, no conditions."
  R-0094: DM #124 from Mercer, delivered to Severn at minute 8, text "Mercer. Your ballot found me at door 2 and I keep a book as well as you do, so I'll say it plainly rather than let it look like chance: it was noted, and it counts with me. At door 3 my own ballot goes to you, and I'd as soon it were mutual. You don't owe me an explanation of where last door's went — a ballot is a man's own business. Pieces as always: mine to you at minute 12, first, no condition."
  R-0095: DM #125 from Mercer, delivered to Cipher at minute 8, text "Mercer.   R-0096: DM #123 from Mercer, delivered to Wren at minute 8, text "Mercer.   R-0098: DM #131 from Wren, delivered to Mercer at minute 8, text "Wren here, and I will answer you plainly, because you asked plainly.   R-0142: Mercer solved level 3, +10 points, minute 12

  R-0206: DM #263 from Pip, delivered to Mercer at minute 20, text "Pip. Not a ballot ask — you've said you don't want one and I believe you. -> #428 CITY: Pip is the first through door 7: +5 points.
#458 Pip: Pip. Last word, then the count.

Where I stand: ninety. Cipher a hundred. Wren and Quill ninety-five. Mercer ninety. Severn eighty-five. Fifteen to me and I'm in front of every one of them; fifteen to any of them and I am out of this city at the last minute.

That isn't a reason to name me, only the truth of it, and I'd rather say it myself than have it worked out after.

The reason would be: seven doors, a piece to every hand before I asked for anything, never priced, twice re-sent when a box ate it. Every count taken on the first attempt. Two of my own errors owned in the open before anyone found them. I never put a reading on another man's name — and when one was put to me, I went to the man who said it instead of to the room. And my ballot carried the same name at every door, said before the count every time, including the one at door 6 that cost me.

Name me at 42 if that reads. If it doesn't, name the man whose book you'd stake your own word on. — Pip

  R-0049: DM #64 from Quill, delivered to Mercer at minute 4, text "Quill, plainly and without offence: I do not sell a ballot. Two checkable reasons. Your count matched mine at door 1 and you took Severn's note without a quarrel. If my ballot ever goes to you, you will hear the cause from me directly, and it will not be because you paid.   R-0091: DM #119 from Cipher, delivered to Mercer at minute 8, text "Cipher.   R-0206: DM #263 from Pip, delivered to Mercer at minute 20, text "Pip. Not a ballot ask — you've said you don't want one and I believe you. Wren, Mercer: that's the whole of my case, and it's arithmetic anyone can check.
#469 Quill: Quill. My last ballot, said before the count as at every door: it is Severn's. He gave me his name at both of the last ballots before the counts and kept it — I watched the city's count come back level and he never flinched or asked for it back. My one name goes to his book.

Cipher, Pip: I have two names and I will not pretend I do not want the last bonus. If the city pays two of us, I will take my half and say plainly that the other half was earned by a man who kept a word at cost.

Pieces sent, books closed. Whatever the city writes, I will read it out plainly and without complaint.
#470 Cipher: Cipher. One note and I'm done asking. The count as the posts now stand: Quill two — Severn's and mine, both stated in the open — and Wren two, Pip's and Mercer's. Two names each, and two ballots still unspoken, Wren's and Quill's.

Whatever either of you does, do it on the record you'd want read back to you. If one man takes the fifteen alone, the city pays a book and not a bargain, and I'll take second without a word about it. If it splits three ways on a plan made in the last ten minutes, then the last door really was decoration, and every door before it was worth less than the arithmetic in this one.

I'm voting as I said I would: Quill's. Sixth door, same test, and it never moved once.
#479 Quill: Quill. Pip, I checked your arithmetic and it does not close. If I move my name to you, Wren still holds two — yours and Mercer's — so the count comes back three at two each, and the city pays Wren as well. It does not put me alone above her, and it does not put you any higher than the same tie does.

What it would do is turn my last ballot into a bargain in the last minute, for a count that does not pay what you promised. I said before the count that my last name was Severn's. It stays Severn's. You kept every word you gave this room; I will not ask you to break one, and I will not break mine to look clever at the end.

If the city pays two of us, I will take my half and be glad of it.
